# `fung fmt -` and `fung fmt --check -`; a `format case` keeps its input as `stdin` (crates/fung-cli/tests/tools_expected.rs).
# $REPO stands for the repository, $DIR for the test's scratch directory.
# Update: FUNG_EXPECTED=bless cargo test -p fung-cli --test tools_expected
==== format case "a+b*c"
-- stdin: a+b*c
-- stdout
| a + b * c
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "x=1"
-- stdin: x=1
-- stdout
| x = 1
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "f( a,b )"
-- stdin: f( a,b )
-- stdout
| f(a, b)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "xs [ 0 ]"
-- stdin: xs [ 0 ]
-- stdout
| xs[0]
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "m [ \"k\" ]"
-- stdin: m [ "k" ]
-- stdout
| m["k"]
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "f(x)(y)"
-- stdin: f(x)(y)
-- stdout
| f(x)(y)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "{ a : 1 , b:2 }"
-- stdin: { a : 1 , b:2 }
-- stdout
| {a: 1, b: 2}
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "{a, b}"
-- stdin: {a, b}
-- stdout
| {a, b}
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "[ 1,2 ]"
-- stdin: [ 1,2 ]
-- stdout
| [1, 2]
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "( 1, )"
-- stdin: ( 1, )
-- stdout
| (1,)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "()"
-- stdin: ()
-- stdout
| ()
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "m . a . b"
-- stdin: m . a . b
-- stdout
| m.a.b
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "0 .. n"
-- stdin: 0 .. n
-- stdout
| 0..n
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "0..=10"
-- stdin: 0..=10
-- stdout
| 0..=10
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "[ a, ... rest ] = xs"
-- stdin: [ a, ... rest ] = xs
-- stdout
| [a, ...rest] = xs
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "x=>x+1"
-- stdin: x=>x+1
-- stdout
| x => x + 1
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "(a,b)=>a"
-- stdin: (a,b)=>a
-- stdout
| (a, b) => a
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "()=>work()"
-- stdin: ()=>work()
-- stdout
| () => work()
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fs.read(p) !"
-- stdin: fs.read(p) !
-- stdout
| fs.read(p)!
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "parse(s) ?"
-- stdin: parse(s) ?
-- stdout
| parse(s)?
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "x ?? y"
-- stdin: x ?? y
-- stdout
| x ?? y
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "xs|>map(f)|>sum"
-- stdin: xs|>map(f)|>sum
-- stdout
| xs |> map(f) |> sum
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "- x"
-- stdin: - x
-- stdout
| -x
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "a - b"
-- stdin: a - b
-- stdout
| a - b
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "a - -b"

==== format case "not  x"

-- stdout

-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "if(a==b): 1 ;;"
-- stdin: if(a==b): 1 ;;
-- stdout
| if (a == b): 1 ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn add(a,b)=a+b"
-- stdin: fn add(a,b)=a+b
-- stdout
| fn add(a, b) = a + b
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn f(x:int)->bool=true"
-- stdin: fn f(x:int)->bool=true
-- stdout
| fn f(x: int) -> bool = true
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn ( x ) : x ;;"
-- stdin: fn ( x ) : x ;;
-- stdout
| fn(x): x ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "each(xs, fn(x): x ;;)"
-- stdin: each(xs, fn(x): x ;;)
-- stdout
| each(xs, fn(x): x ;;)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "exec(cmd, cwd : d)"
-- stdin: exec(cmd, cwd : d)
-- stdout
| exec(cmd, cwd: d)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "match x: 0=>1;_=>2;;"
-- stdin: match x: 0=>1;_=>2;;
-- stdout
| match x: 0 => 1; _ => 2 ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "match v: 1|2 => 'a ;;"
-- stdin: match v: 1|2 => 'a ;;
-- stdout
| match v: 1 | 2 => 'a ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "Ok( x )"
-- stdin: Ok( x )
-- stdout
| Ok(x)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "c then a else b"
-- stdin: c then a else b
-- stdout
| c then a else b
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "x in [1, 2]"
-- stdin: x in [1, 2]
-- stdout
| x in [1, 2]
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "\"a ${ x+1 } b\""
-- stdin: "a ${ x+1 } b"
-- stdout
| "a ${ x+1 } b"
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "`raw  text`"
-- stdin: `raw  text`
-- stdout
| `raw  text`
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "1_000 + 0xff + 2 ** 10"
-- stdin: 1_000 + 0xff + 2 ** 10
-- stdout
| 1_000 + 0xff + 2 ** 10
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "a;b"
-- stdin: a;b
-- stdout
| a; b
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn f(): a ;;"
-- stdin: fn f(): a ;;
-- stdout
| fn f(): a ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "x   \n"
-- stdin
| x   
-- stdout
| x
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case ""
-- stdin
-- stdout
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn f(x):\nx + 1\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| fn f(x):
| x + 1
| ;;
-- stdout
| fn f(x):
|   x + 1
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn f(x):\n    if x:\n  1\n  ;;\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| fn f(x):
|     if x:
|   1
|   ;;
| ;;
-- stdout
| fn f(x):
|   if x:
|     1
|   ;;
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "if c:\na\nelse:\nb\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| if c:
| a
| else:
| b
| ;;
-- stdout
| if c:
|   a
| else:
|   b
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "if c:\na\nelse if d:\nb\nelse:\nc\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| if c:
| a
| else if d:
| b
| else:
| c
| ;;
-- stdout
| if c:
|   a
| else if d:
|   b
| else:
|   c
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "test \"t\":\nassert true\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| test "t":
| assert true
| ;;
-- stdout
| test "t":
|   assert true
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "for x in xs:\nprint(x)\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| for x in xs:
| print(x)
| ;;
-- stdout
| for x in xs:
|   print(x)
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "x = c then a\nelse b\n"
-- stdin
| x = c then a
| else b
-- stdout
| x = c then a
|   else b
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "if c:\n  x = d then 1\n  else 2\nelse:\n  3\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| if c:
|   x = d then 1
|   else 2
| else:
|   3
| ;;
-- stdout
| if c:
|   x = d then 1
|     else 2
| else:
|   3
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "match v:\nOk(x) =>\nx\nErr(e) => 0\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| match v:
| Ok(x) =>
| x
| Err(e) => 0
| ;;
-- stdout
| match v:
|   Ok(x)  =>
|     x
|   Err(e) => 0
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "match v:\n  Ok(x) => f(y => y)\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| match v:
|   Ok(x) => f(y => y)
| ;;
-- stdout
| match v:
|   Ok(x) => f(y => y)
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "f(a,\nb,\nc)\n"
-- stdin
| f(a,
| b,
| c)
-- stdout
| f(a,
|   b,
|   c)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "xs = [\n1,\n2,\n]\n"
-- stdin
| xs = [
| 1,
| 2,
| ]

| xs = [
|   1,
|   2,
| ]
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "m = {\na: 1,\n}\n"
-- stdin
| m = {
| a: 1,
| }
-- stdout

|   a: 1,
| }
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "each(xs, fn(x):\nprint(x)\n;;)\n"
-- stdin
| each(xs, fn(x):
| print(x)
| ;;)
-- stdout
| each(xs, fn(x):
|   print(x)
| ;;)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn g():\neach(xs, fn(x):\nprint(x)\n;;)\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| fn g():
| each(xs, fn(x):
| print(x)
| ;;)
| ;;
-- stdout
| fn g():
|   each(xs, fn(x):
|     print(x)
|   ;;)
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "xs\n|> map(f)\n|> sum\n"
-- stdin
| xs
| |> map(f)
| |> sum
-- stdout
| xs
|   |> map(f)
|   |> sum
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== format case "fn f(x: int) -> int = x\ny = 1\n"

==== format case "fn f(a) = if a: 1 else: 2 ;;\nmatch x:\n0 => 1\n;;\n"

| 0 => 1
| ;;
-- stdout

|   0 => 1
| ;;

-- exit: 0

==== format case "fn f(h: fn):\nif h:\n1\nelse:\n2\n;;\n;;\nx=1\n"
-- stdin
| fn f(h: fn):
| if h:
| 1
| else:
| 2
| ;;
| ;;
| x=1
-- stdout
| fn f(h: fn):
|   if h:
|     1
|   else:
|     2
|   ;;
| ;;
| x = 1
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn g(h: fn):\neach(xs, fn(x):\nh(x)\n;;)\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| fn g(h: fn):
| each(xs, fn(x):
| h(x)
| ;;)
| ;;
-- stdout
| fn g(h: fn):
|   each(xs, fn(x):
|     h(x)
|   ;;)
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn f(h: fn, x: int) = h(x)\ny = 1\n"
-- stdin
| fn f(h: fn, x: int) = h(x)
| y = 1
-- stdout
| fn f(h: fn, x: int) = h(x)
| y = 1
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn f() -> fn:\nprint\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| fn f() -> fn:
| print
| ;;
-- stdout
| fn f() -> fn:
|   print
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn f(h: fn | nil, g: list[fn]):\nh\n;;\n"
-- stdin
| fn f(h: fn | nil, g: list[fn]):
| h
| ;;
-- stdout
| fn f(h: fn | nil, g: list[fn]):
|   h
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "apply(fn(x): x ;;, fn(y):\ny\n;;)\nz = 1\n"
-- stdin
| apply(fn(x): x ;;, fn(y):
| y
| ;;)
| z = 1
-- stdout
| apply(fn(x): x ;;, fn(y):
|   y
| ;;)
| z = 1
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== format case "fn g():\nh = fn(x: fn):\nx()\n;;\nh\n;;\n"

| h = fn(x: fn):
| x()
| ;;
| h
| ;;
-- stdout

|   h = fn(x: fn):
|     x()
|   ;;
|   h
| ;;

-- exit: 0

==== manual index.md:2
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # usage: fung biggest.fung <dir> [n]
| import fs
| import path
|
| dir = first(args) ?? fail("usage: biggest.fung <dir> [n]")
| n = int(get_arg(1, "5"))
|
| fn get_arg(i, default) = i < len(args) then args[i] else default
|
| sizes = fs.list(dir)!
|   |> map(f => (f, fs.stat(path.join(dir, f))!.size))
|   |> sort_by(((_, size)) => -size)
|   |> take(n)
|
| for (name, size) in sizes:
|   print(pad_left(str(size), 10), name)
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual index.md:3
-- stdout
| fn slug(s) = lower(s) |> split(" ") |> filter(w => w != "") |> join("-")
|
| test "slug joins words with dashes":
|   assert slug("Hello  World") == "hello-world"
|   assert slug("") == ""
| ;;

==== manual learn/06-strings.md:12
-- stdout
| fn width(s) = chars(s) |> map(c => "가" <= c and c <= "힣" then 2 else 1) |> sum
| fn pad_cols(s, n) = s + repeat(" ", max([0, n - width(s)]))
|
| for name in ["web-1", "데이터베이스", "캐시-1"]:
|   print(pad_cols(name, 14) + "| up")
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

-- stdout
| log = ["INFO start", "ERROR disk full", "WARN slow", "ERROR timeout", "INFO done"]
| fn level(line) = split(line, " ")[0]
| counts = log |> fold({}, (acc, l) => put(acc, level(l), get(acc, level(l), 0) + 1))
| print(counts)

==== manual learn/07-collections.md:17
-- stdout
| specs = ["web-1:80", "db-1:5432", "cache-1:6379"]
| fn add_spec(m, spec):
|   parts = split(spec, ":")
|   put(m, parts[0], int(parts[1]))
| ;;
| ports = specs |> fold({}, add_spec)
| print(ports)
| print(ports["db-1"])

-- exit: 0
==== manual learn/07-collections.md:18
-- stdout

| slow = checks

| fn status_text(code) = match code:
|   200 => "OK"
|   404 => "Not Found"
|   500 => "Internal Server Error"
|   _   => "status ${code}"
| ;;

| print(status_text(404))

==== manual learn/08-patterns.md:3
-- stdout
| fn describe(x) = match x:
|   0           => "zero"
|   -1          => "minus one"
|   "yes" | "y" => "agreed"
|   'ok         => "the symbol ok"
|   true        => "true"
|   nil         => "nothing"
|   1s          => "one second"
|   _           => "something else"
| ;;
|
| print(describe(0), describe(-1), describe("y"), describe('ok))
| print(describe(true), describe(nil), describe(1s), describe(3.5))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual learn/08-patterns.md:4
-- stdout
| fn summary(hosts) = match hosts:
|   []                => "no hosts"
|   [only]            => "one host: ${only}"
|   [a, b]            => "two hosts: ${a}, ${b}"
|   [head, ...others] => "${head} and ${len(others)} more"
| ;;
|
| print(summary([]))
| print(summary(["web1"]))
| print(summary(["web1", "web2"]))
| print(summary(["web1", "web2", "db1", "db2"]))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual learn/08-patterns.md:5
-- stdout
| fn run_command(words) = match words:
|   ["start", name]       => "starting ${name}"
|   ["stop", name]        => "stopping ${name}"
|   ["restart", ...names] => "restarting ${join(names, ", ")}"
|   _                     => "unknown command: ${join(words, " ")}"
| ;;
|
| print(run_command(split("start api", " ")))
| print(run_command(split("restart api worker", " ")))
| print(run_command(split("delete everything", " ")))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual learn/08-patterns.md:6
-- stdout
| events = [
|   {kind: "deploy", service: "api", version: "1.4.2"},
|   {kind: "alert", level: 3, text: "disk 95%"},
|   {kind: "alert", level: 1, text: "slow query"},
|   {kind: "login", user: "kim"},
| ]
|
| for e in events:
|   match e:
|     {kind: "deploy", service, version} => print("deployed ${service} ${version}")
|     {kind: "alert", level: 3, text}    => print("PAGE: ${text}")
|     {kind: "alert", text}              => print("note: ${text}")
|     {kind}                             => print("ignored ${kind}")
|   ;;
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual learn/08-patterns.md:7
-- stdout
| fn first_port(svc) = match svc:
|   {name, ports: [p, ...]} => "${name} listens on ${p}"
|   {name, ports: []}       => "${name} has no ports"
| ;;
|
| print(first_port({name: "api", ports: [8080, 8443]}))
| print(first_port({name: "batch", ports: []}))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual learn/08-patterns.md:8
-- stdout
| fn route(method, path) = match (method, path):
|   ("GET", "/health") => "health check"
|   ("GET", p)         => "read ${p}"
|   ("POST", p)        => "write ${p}"
|   (m, _)             => "method ${m} not allowed"
| ;;
|
| print(route("GET", "/health"))
| print(route("GET", "/users"))
| print(route("DELETE", "/users"))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual learn/08-patterns.md:9
-- stdout
| fn classify(svc) = match svc:
|   {port} when port < 1024  => "${svc.name}: system port"
|   {port} when port > 49151 => "${svc.name}: dynamic port"
|   {port}                   => "${svc.name}: registered port ${port}"
| ;;
|
| print(classify({name: "ssh", port: 22}))
| print(classify({name: "api", port: 8080}))
| print(classify({name: "tmp", port: 60000}))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

| fn distance((x1, y1), (x2, y2)) = (x2 - x1) ** 2 + (y2 - y1) ** 2
|
| print(distance((0, 0), (3, 4)))

| limits = {cpu: "500m", memory: "256Mi"}

|   print("${key}=${value}")
| ;;

==== manual learn/08-patterns.md:14
-- stdout
| fn area(shape) = match shape:
|   Circle(r)  => 3.14 * r * r
|   Rect(w, h) => w * h
|   Square(s)  => s * s
| ;;
|
| shapes = [Circle(1), Rect(2, 5), Square(3)]
| print(shapes)
| print(shapes |> map(area))
| print(type_of(Rect(2, 5)), Circle(1) == Circle(1))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual learn/08-patterns.md:15
-- stdout
| fn hex(color) = match color:
|   Red   => "#ff0000"
|   Green => "#00ff00"
|   Blue  => "#0000ff"
| ;;
|
| print(hex(Red), hex(Blue))
| print(Red == 'Red, type_of(Red))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual learn/08-patterns.md:16
-- stdout
| fn triage(line) = match split(line, " "):
|   ["ERROR", ...words] => "page: ${join(words, " ")}"
|   ["WARN", ...words]  => "ticket: ${join(words, " ")}"
|   _                   => "skip"
| ;;
|
| for line in ["ERROR disk full", "WARN slow", "INFO started", ""]:
|   print(triage(line))
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual learn/08-patterns.md:17
-- stdout
| fn replicas_text(cfg) = match cfg:
|   {name, replicas: 0}                 => "${name}: stopped"
|   {name, replicas: 1}                 => "${name}: single"
|   {name, replicas} when replicas >= 2 => "${name}: ${replicas} replicas"
| ;;
|
| print(replicas_text({name: "api", replicas: 3}))
| print(replicas_text({name: "cron", replicas: 1}))
| print(replicas_text({name: "old", replicas: 0}))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

-- stdout
| fn check_port(p):
|   if p < 1 or p > 65535:
|     return Err({kind: 'bad_port, message: "port out of range: ${p}"})
|   ;;
|   Ok(p)
| ;;
|
| print(check_port(443))
| print(check_port(70000))
| print(check_port(0) ?? 8080)

-- exit: 0

| fn add_args(xs) = match xs:
|   [a, b] => Ok(parse_int(a)? + parse_int(b)?)
|   _      => Err({kind: 'usage, message: "usage: add.fung A B"})
| ;;
|
| match add_args(args):
|   Ok(total) => print(total)
|   Err(e)    =>
|     eprint("error:", e.message)
|     exit(1)
| ;;

-- exit: 0

==== manual learn/09-errors.md:17
-- stdout
| fn parse_kv(line) = match split(line, "="):
|   [k, v] => Ok((k, v))
|   _      => Err({kind: 'syntax, message: "not key=value: ${line}"})
| ;;
|
| fn parse_all(rows) = try_map(rows, parse_kv)
|
| print(parse_all(["port=8080", "host=db"]))
| print(parse_all(["port=8080", "broken", "debug=true"]))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual learn/11-files-and-commands.md:2
-- stdout
| import fs
|
| match fs.read("settings.ini"):

==== manual learn/12-concurrency.md:11
-- stdout
| fn countdown(s, n):
|   (tx, rx) = channel()
|   spawn(s, fn():
|     for i in reverse(1..=n):
|       send(tx, i)!
|     ;;
|   ;;)
|   rx
| ;;
|
| scope(fn(s):
|   for x in countdown(s, 3):
|     print("got", x)
|   ;;
| ;;)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual learn/12-concurrency.md:12
-- stdout
| fn worker(id, jobs, results):
|   for job in jobs:
|     sleep(10ms)
|     send(results, "worker ${id} did job ${job}")!
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| done = scope(fn(s):
|   (jobs_tx, jobs_rx) = channel()
|   (res_tx, res_rx) = channel()
|   each([1, 2], id => spawn(s, () => worker(id, jobs_rx, res_tx)))
|   spawn(s, () => each(1..=4, j => send(jobs_tx, j)!))
|   for r in res_rx: r ;;
| ;;)
| print(len(done), "jobs done")
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

|     print(x)

| ;;)

| rows = zip(hosts, results)

|   status = match r:
|     Ok(s)                 => s
|     Err({kind: 'timeout}) => "TIMEOUT"
|     Err(e)                => "ERROR ${e.message}"
|   ;;
|   print(pad_right(host, 6), status)
| ;;
| if any(results, is_err): exit(1) ;;

|   close(tx)
|   await(counter)
| ;;)

==== manual learn/13-json-and-http.md:10
-- stdout
| import http
| import json
|
| hits = atom(0)
|
| fn handle(req):
|   swap(hits, n => n + 1)
|   match (req.method, req.path):
|     ("GET", "/health") => "ok"
|     ("GET", "/hello")  => "hello, ${get(req.query, "name", "stranger")}"
|     ("GET", "/stats")  => {status: 200, headers: {"content-type": "application/json"}, body: json.stringify({hits: deref(hits)})}
|     _                  => {status: 404, body: "not found: ${req.path}"}
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| http.serve("127.0.0.1:8080", handle, ready: addr => print("listening on", addr))

==== manual learn/14-testing.md:5
-- stdout
| import fs
| import path
|
| # Moves every .log file in dir to <name>.log.1 and returns how many it moved.
| fn rotate(dir):
|   logs = fs.glob(path.join(dir, "*.log"))?
|   try_map(logs, p => fs.move(p, p + ".1", overwrite: true))?
|   Ok(len(logs))
| ;;
|
| test "rotate renames each log":
|   fs.with_temp_dir(fn(d):
|     fs.write(path.join(d, "a.log"), "x")!
|     fs.write(path.join(d, "b.txt"), "y")!
|     assert rotate(d)! == 1
|     assert fs.list(d)! == ["a.log.1", "b.txt"]
|   ;;)
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual learn/14-testing.md:9
-- stdout
| fn one_decimal(x) = int(x * 10) / 10
|
| fn human(n):
|   if n < 1024:
|     "${n} B"
|   else if n < 1024 * 1024:
|     "${one_decimal(n / 1024)} KiB"
|   else:
|     "${one_decimal(n / (1024 * 1024))} MiB"
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| test "bytes, KiB and MiB":
|   assert human(512) == "512 B"
|   assert human(1023) == "1023 B"
|   assert human(1024) == "1.0 KiB"
|   assert human(1536) == "1.5 KiB"
|   assert human(1048576) == "1.0 MiB"
| ;;

==== manual recipes/check-urls.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Checks many URLs at once: status, time taken, and whether the answer
| # was good (below 400, within the time limit). Exit code 1 if any was not.
| # usage: fung checkurls.fung urls.txt [--timeout 5s]
| import conc
| import cli
| import fs
| import http
| import time
|
| fn check(url, limit):
|   t0 = time.now()
|   r = http.get(url, timeout: limit)
|   ms = (time.now() - t0) // 1ms
|   match r:
|     Ok(res) => {url, ok: res.status < 400, status: str(res.status), ms, note: ""}
|     Err(e)  => {url, ok: false, status: "-", ms, note: " (${e.message})"}
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| opts = cli.parse(args)
| file = first(opts.rest) ?? fail("usage: checkurls.fung urls.txt [--timeout 5s]")
| limit = parse_dur(get(opts.flags, "timeout", "5s")) ?? fail("--timeout takes a duration like 5s")
| urls = fs.read_lines(file)! |> map(trim) |> filter(u => u != "" and not starts_with(u, "#"))
|
| results = conc.pmap(urls, u => check(u, limit), limit: 16)
| for r in results:
|   mark = r.ok then "ok  " else "FAIL"
|   print(mark, pad_left(r.status, 3), pad_left("${r.ms}ms", 7), r.url + r.note)
| ;;
| bad = results |> filter(r => not r.ok) |> len
| eprint("${len(results) - bad} ok, ${bad} failed")
| if bad > 0: exit(1) ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual recipes/cli-options.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # prune: keeps the newest N backups in a directory and deletes the rest.
| import fs
| import path
| import cli
|
| help = `usage: prune.fung [options] DIR
|
| Keeps the newest backups in DIR and deletes the older ones. Backups are
| the files matching --pattern; their names sort by date (db-2026-10-19.tar.gz).
|
| options:
|   --keep N         how many to keep (default 7)
|   --pattern GLOB   which files are backups (default *.tar.gz)
|   --dry-run        only print what would be deleted
|   -h, --help       show this help`
|
| known = ["keep", "pattern", "dry_run", "help"]
|
| # A mistake in the arguments: say what is wrong, point at --help, exit 2.
| fn usage_error(msg):
|   eprint("prune.fung: ${msg}")
|   eprint("try `prune.fung --help`")
|   exit(2)
| ;;
|
| opts = cli.parse(args, bools: ["dry-run", "help"])
| if get(opts.flags, "help", false) or "-h" in opts.rest:
|   print(help)
|   exit(0)
| ;;
| unknown = keys(opts.flags) |> filter(k => k not in known)
| if not is_empty(unknown): usage_error("unknown option --${replace(unknown[0], "_", "-")}") ;;
|
| dir = match opts.rest:
|   [d] => d
|   []  => usage_error("DIR is missing")
|   _   => usage_error("only one DIR, got ${len(opts.rest)}")
| ;;
| keep = match parse_int(get(opts.flags, "keep", "7")):
|   Ok(n) when n >= 1 => n
|   _                 => usage_error("--keep takes a whole number of at least 1")
| ;;
| pattern = get(opts.flags, "pattern", "*.tar.gz")
| dry = get(opts.flags, "dry_run", false)
|
| backups = fs.glob(path.join(dir, pattern))! |> sort |> reverse
| old = drop(backups, keep)
| for f in old:
|   print(dry then "would delete" else "delete", f)
|   if not dry: fs.remove(f)! ;;
| ;;
| print("kept ${len(backups) - len(old)}, ${dry then "would delete" else "deleted"} ${len(old)}")
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual recipes/count-log-patterns.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Counts the levels in many log files and the most frequent errors.
| # Lines look like "2026-10-20 09:01:13 ERROR upstream timeout after 3000 ms".
| # usage: fung logcount.fung [PATTERN] [--top N]      (PATTERN defaults to "*.log")
| import fs
| import cli
| import regex
|
| levels = ["INFO", "WARN", "ERROR"]
|
| # One line → {level, message}, or nil when it is not a log line.
| fn parse(line) = match split(line, " "):
|   [_, _, level, ...words] when level in levels => {level, message: join(words, " ")}
|   _                                            => nil
| ;;
|
| # Numbers differ from line to line; "after 3000 ms" and "after 5000 ms"
| # are the same problem.
| fn shape(message) = regex.replace(message, `\b\d+\b`, "N")
|
| opts = cli.parse(args)
| pattern = first(opts.rest) ?? "*.log"
| top = parse_int(get(opts.flags, "top", "3")) ?? fail("--top must be a number")
| files = fs.glob(pattern)!
| if is_empty(files): fail("no files match ${pattern}") ;;
|
| # file → its parsed entries
| logs = files |> map(f => (f, fs.read_lines(f)! |> map(parse) |> filter(e => e != nil)))
|
| print(pad_right("file", 10), levels |> map(l => pad_left(l, 6)) |> join(" "))
| for (file, entries) in logs:
|   counts = group_by(entries, e => e.level)
|   cells = levels |> map(l => pad_left(str(len(get(counts, l, []))), 6))
|   print(pad_right(file, 10), join(cells, " "))
| ;;
|
| errors = logs
|   |> map(((_, entries)) => entries)
|   |> flatten
|   |> filter(e => e.level == "ERROR")
|   |> group_by(e => shape(e.message))
|   |> pairs
|   |> map(((msg, es)) => (len(es), msg))
|   |> sort_by(((n, _)) => -n)
|   |> take(top)
| print()
| print("top errors:")
| for (n, msg) in errors:
|   print(pad_left(str(n), 4), msg)
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual recipes/exit-codes.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # A monitoring check (Nagios, Icinga, Sensu): counts ERROR lines in a log.
| # One status line on standard output; the exit code is the verdict:
| #   0 OK, 1 WARNING, 2 CRITICAL, 3 UNKNOWN (the check itself could not run)
| # usage: fung check_errors.fung LOG [--warn 1] [--crit 10]
| import cli
| import fs
|
| fn finish(code, text):
|   names = ["OK", "WARNING", "CRITICAL", "UNKNOWN"]
|   print("${names[code]} - ${text}")
|   exit(code)
| ;;
|
| fn number(opts, name, default) = match parse_int(get(opts.flags, name, str(default))):
|   Ok(n)  => n
|   Err(_) => finish(3, "--${name} is not a number")
| ;;
|
| opts = cli.parse(args)
| file = first(opts.rest) ?? finish(3, "usage: check_errors.fung LOG [--warn N] [--crit N]")
| warn = number(opts, "warn", 1)
| crit = number(opts, "crit", 10)
|
| log_lines = match fs.read_lines(file):
|   Ok(ls) => ls
|   Err(e) => finish(3, e.message)
| ;;
| errors = log_lines |> filter(l => " ERROR " in l) |> len
| eprint("debug: ${len(log_lines)} lines read from ${file}")
|
| text = "${errors} errors in ${file} | errors=${errors};${warn};${crit}"
| if errors >= crit: finish(2, text) ;;
| if errors >= warn: finish(1, text) ;;
| finish(0, text)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual recipes/find-big-files.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Lists the largest files under a directory, largest first.
| # usage: fung bigfiles.fung [dir] [--top N] [--min SIZE]   (SIZE: 500, 10K, 2M, 1G)
| import fs
| import cli
|
| # "10K" → 10240. A plain number is bytes.
| fn parse_size(s):
|   (num, unit) = match upper(s[-1..]):
|     "K" => (s[..-1], 1024)
|     "M" => (s[..-1], 1024 * 1024)
|     "G" => (s[..-1], 1024 * 1024 * 1024)
|     _   => (s, 1)
|   ;;
|   (parse_int(num) ?? fail("not a size: ${s}")) * unit
| ;;
|
| # 1536 → "1.5K".
| fn human(n):
|   n < 1024 then "${n}B"
|     else n < 1024 * 1024 then "${n * 10 // 1024 / 10}K"
|     else "${n * 10 // (1024 * 1024) / 10}M"
| ;;
|
| # A file that vanished or cannot be read since the walk counts as empty.
| fn size_of(p) = match fs.stat(p):
|   Ok(st) => st.size
|   Err(_) => 0
| ;;
|
| opts = cli.parse(args)
| dir = first(opts.rest) ?? "."
| top = parse_int(get(opts.flags, "top", "10")) ?? fail("--top must be a number")
| min_size = parse_size(get(opts.flags, "min", "0"))
|
| found = fs.walk(dir)!
|   |> map(p => {path: p, size: size_of(p)})
|   |> filter(f => f.size >= min_size)
|   |> sort_by(f => -f.size)
|   |> take(top)
| for f in found:
|   print(pad_left(human(f.size), 7), f.path)
| ;;
| print(pad_left(human(found |> map(f => f.size) |> sum), 7), "total")
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual recipes/json-fields.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Prints the services of a deploy API as a table: name, state, replicas
| # ready/wanted, team. Reads the JSON from a URL, or from standard input:
| #   fung services.fung https://deploy.example.com/api/v1/services
| #   curl -s https://deploy.example.com/api/v1/services | fung services.fung
| # --state STATE keeps only the services in that state.
| import cli
| import http
| import json
|
| fn fetch(url):
|   res = http.get(url, headers: {accept: "application/json"}, timeout: 10s)?
|   if res.status != 200: return Err({kind: 'http, message: "${url}: HTTP ${res.status}"}) ;;
|   Ok(res.body)
| ;;
|
| # The value at a path of keys, or nil when any step is missing.
| fn dig(v, path) = match path:
|   []           => v
|   [k, ...rest] => type_of(v) == 'map then dig(get(v, k), rest) else nil
| ;;
|
| opts = cli.parse(args)
| text = match first(opts.rest):
|   nil => join(read_lines()!, "\n")
|   url => fetch(url)!
| ;;
| items = dig(json.parse(text)!, ["items"]) ?? []
| wanted = get(opts.flags, "state")
|
| rows = items
|   |> filter(s => wanted == nil or dig(s, ["status", "state"]) == wanted)
|   |> map(s => [
|     s.name,
|     dig(s, ["status", "state"]) ?? "?",
|     "${dig(s, ["status", "replicas", "ready"]) ?? 0}/${dig(s, ["status", "replicas", "want"]) ?? 0}",
|     dig(s, ["labels", "team"]) ?? "-",
|   ])
| for [name, state, ready, team] in rows:
|   print(pad_right(name, 8), pad_right(state, 9), pad_left(ready, 5), " ", team)
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual recipes/pagination.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Follows a paged JSON API to the end and prints every item as one JSON
| # line. Each page is {"items": [...], "next": URL or null}.
| # usage: fung allpages.fung https://inventory.example.com/api/hosts > hosts.jsonl
| import http
| import json
|
| max_pages = 1000
| token = env("API_TOKEN")
| headers = token == nil then {accept: "application/json"}
|   else {accept: "application/json", authorization: "Bearer ${token}"}
|
| # One page as a map. A 429 (too many requests) waits as the server asks
| # and tries the same page again.
| fn page(url, tries = 1):
|   res = http.get(url, headers: headers, timeout: 30s)?
|   match res.status:
|     200                => json.parse(res.body)
|     429 when tries < 5 =>
|       wait = parse_int(get(res.headers, "retry-after", "1")) ?? 1
|       eprint("rate limited, waiting ${wait}s")
|       sleep(wait * 1s)
|       page(url, tries + 1)
|     code               => Err({kind: 'http, message: "${url}: HTTP ${code}"})
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| # All the items from url on: pages are fetched one after another, each
| # naming the next. Returns Ok(items) or the first Err.
| fn all_items(url, acc, n):
|   if n > max_pages: return Err({kind: 'limit, message: "more than ${max_pages} pages"}) ;;
|   p = page(url)?
|   items = acc + get(p, "items", [])
|   match get(p, "next"):
|     nil  => Ok(items)
|     next => all_items(next, items, n + 1)
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| url = first(args) ?? fail("usage: allpages.fung URL")
| items = all_items(url, [], 1)!
| each(items, i => print(json.stringify(i)))
| eprint("${len(items)} items")
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

| print(until(1, x => x * 2, x => x > 100))

==== manual recipes/regex-split.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Reads node_exporter metrics (Prometheus text format) on standard input
| # and prints how full each filesystem is, fullest first. Exit code 1 if
| # any is above --max percent (default 90).
| # usage: curl -s http://web1:9100/metrics | fung diskfull.fung [--max 90]
| import cli
| import regex
|
| # name{labels} value   (the labels are optional)
| sample = `^(?P<name>[a-zA-Z_:][\w:]*)(\{(?P<labels>.*)\})?\s+(?P<value>\S+)$`
| # key="value" pairs inside the braces
| label = `(\w+)="((?:[^"\\]|\\.)*)"`
|
| # One line → {name, labels: {…}, value}, or nil for comments, blanks and
| # values that are not numbers (NaN, +Inf).
| fn parse(line):
|   m = regex.find(line, sample)
|   if m == nil: return nil ;;
|   labels = regex.find_all(m.named.labels ?? "", label)
|     |> fold({}, (acc, l) => put(acc, l.groups[0], l.groups[1]))
|   match parse_float(m.named.value):
|     Ok(v)  => {name: m.named.name, labels, value: v}
|     Err(_) => nil
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| # The samples of one metric as mountpoint → value.
| fn by_mount(samples, name) = samples
|   |> filter(s => s.name == name and "mountpoint" in s.labels)
|   |> filter(s => get(s.labels, "fstype", "") != "tmpfs")
|   |> fold({}, (acc, s) => put(acc, s.labels.mountpoint, s.value))
|
| opts = cli.parse(args)
| max_pct = parse_int(get(opts.flags, "max", "90")) ?? fail("--max takes a whole percent")
| samples = read_lines()! |> map(parse) |> filter(s => s != nil)
| avail = by_mount(samples, "node_filesystem_avail_bytes")
| size = by_mount(samples, "node_filesystem_size_bytes")
|
| rows = keys(size)
|   |> filter(mp => mp in avail and size[mp] > 0)
|   |> map(mp => (mp, int(100 - avail[mp] * 100 / size[mp]), size[mp] / 1024 ** 3))
|   |> sort_by(((_, pct, _)) => -pct)
| for (mp, pct, gib) in rows:
|   flag = pct > max_pct then "  <-- over ${max_pct}%" else ""
|   print(pad_right(mp, 8), pad_left("${pct}%", 4), pad_left("${int(gib)} GiB", 8) + flag)
| ;;
| if any(rows, ((_, pct, _)) => pct > max_pct): exit(1) ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual recipes/rename.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Renames the files in a directory to kebab-case:
| #   "Q3 Report (final).TXT" -> "q3-report-final.txt"
| # usage: fung rename.fung <dir> [--dry-run]
| import fs
| import path
| import cli
|
| # Lowercase, letters and digits kept, every other run of characters one "-".
| fn kebab(name):
|   kept = for c in chars(lower(name)):
|     c in "abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789" then c else "-"
|   ;;
|   join(kept) |> split("-") |> filter(p => p != "") |> join("-")
| ;;
|
| # The new name; a name with no ASCII letter or digit is kept.
| fn target(file):
|   stem = kebab(path.stem(file))
|   stem == "" then file else stem + lower(path.ext(file))
| ;;
|
| fn rename(dir, file, dry):
|   to = target(file)
|   if to == file: return 'same ;;
|   if fs.exists(path.join(dir, to)):
|     eprint("skip:", file, "->", to, "(already exists)")
|     return 'skipped
|   ;;
|   print(file, "->", to)
|   if not dry: fs.move(path.join(dir, file), path.join(dir, to))! ;;
|   'renamed
| ;;
|
| opts = cli.parse(args, bools: ["dry-run"])
| dir = first(opts.rest) ?? fail("usage: rename.fung <dir> [--dry-run]")
| dry = get(opts.flags, "dry_run", false)
| results = fs.list(dir)! |> map(f => rename(dir, f, dry))
| done = results |> filter(r => r == 'renamed) |> len
| skipped = results |> filter(r => r == 'skipped) |> len
| note = dry then " (dry run)" else ""
| print("${done} renamed, ${skipped} skipped${note}")
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual recipes/replace-config.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Sets KEY=VALUE pairs in a "key = value" config file, keeping comments,
| # spacing and order. Keys that are not there are added at the end.
| # usage: fung setconf.fung <file> KEY=VALUE... [--print]
| import fs
| import cli
| import regex
|
| # indent, key, separator, value; comment lines never match
| setting = `^(\s*)([\w.-]+)(\s*=\s*)(.*)$`
|
| # "port=9090" → ("port", "9090"); the value may itself hold "=".
| fn pair(arg):
|   [k, ...rest] = split(arg, "=")
|   if k == "" or is_empty(rest): fail("not KEY=VALUE: ${arg}") ;;
|   (k, join(rest, "="))
| ;;
|
| # The line with its key's new value, or the line as it was.
| fn update(line, wanted):
|   m = regex.find(line, setting)
|   if m == nil: return line ;;
|   [indent, key, sep, old] = m.groups
|   new = get(wanted, key, old)
|   if new != old: eprint("${key}: ${old} -> ${new}") ;;
|   indent + key + sep + new
| ;;
|
| opts = cli.parse(args, bools: ["print"])
| file = first(opts.rest) ?? fail("usage: setconf.fung <file> KEY=VALUE... [--print]")
| wanted = drop(opts.rest, 1) |> map(pair) |> fold({}, (m, (k, v)) => put(m, k, v))
|
| old = fs.read_lines(file)!
| existing = old |> map(l => regex.find(l, setting)) |> filter(m => m != nil) |> map(m => m.groups[1])
| added = keys(wanted) |> filter(k => k not in existing) |> map(k => "${k}=${wanted[k]}")
| result = (old |> map(l => update(l, wanted))) + added
| each(added, l => eprint("added:", l))
|
| if get(opts.flags, "print", false):
|   print(join(result, "\n"))
| else:
|   # a whole new file first, then one rename: never half a config
|   fs.write(file + ".tmp", join(result, "\n") + "\n")!
|   fs.move(file + ".tmp", file, overwrite: true)!
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual recipes/save-csv-json.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Turns JSON Lines (one object per line) into a CSV file and one JSON
| # file. Missing fields and null become empty cells.
| # usage: fung save.fung hosts.jsonl report   (writes report.csv, report.json)
| import fs
| import json
|
| columns = ["id", "name", "role", "disk", "note"]
|
| # One CSV cell: quoted when it holds a comma, a quote or a line break,
| # with quotes doubled inside.
| fn cell(v):
|   s = v == nil then "" else type_of(v) == 'str then v else str(v)
|   quote = any([",", "\"", "\n", "\r"], c => c in s)
|   quote then "\"" + replace(s, "\"", "\"\"") + "\"" else s
| ;;
|
| fn csv(rows) = ([columns] + map(rows, r => map(columns, c => get(r, c))))
|   |> map(cells => cells |> map(cell) |> join(","))
|   |> map(line => line + "\n")
|   |> join
|
| (src, out) = match args:
|   [a, b] => (a, b)
|   _      => fail("usage: save.fung input.jsonl output-name")
| ;;
| rows = (fs.read_lines(src)! |> filter(l => trim(l) != "") |> try_map(json.parse))!
|
| fs.write("${out}.csv", csv(rows))!
| fs.write("${out}.json", json.stringify(rows, indent: 2) + "\n")!
| print("wrote ${out}.csv and ${out}.json: ${len(rows)} rows")
|
| # show what was written
| print(trim(fs.read("${out}.csv")!))
| print(trim(fs.read("${out}.json")!))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual recipes/server-table.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Asks every server for a few facts in one ssh call each and prints one
| # table: a row per server, a column per fact.
| # usage: fung inventory.fung hosts.txt
| import conc
| import fs
|
| # Runs on the server (its own shell) and prints key=value lines.
| probe = `echo kernel=$(uname -r)
| echo cpus=$(nproc)
| echo load=$(cut -d' ' -f1 /proc/loadavg)
| df -P / | awk 'NR == 2 {print "disk=" $5}'`
|
| columns = ["kernel", "cpus", "load", "disk"]
|
| # "a=1\nb=2" → {a: "1", b: "2"}; lines without "=" are skipped.
| fn parse_facts(text) = lines(text)
|   |> map(l => split(l, "="))
|   |> filter(kv => len(kv) >= 2)
|   |> fold({}, (m, [k, ...v]) => put(m, trim(k), trim(join(v, "="))))
|
| # One server → its row: the facts, or dashes and why it failed.
| fn row(host):
|   r = timeout(20s, () => exec(["ssh", "-o", "BatchMode=yes", "-o", "ConnectTimeout=5", host, probe]))
|   match r:
|     Ok(res) =>
|       facts = parse_facts(res.out)
|       [host] + map(columns, c => get(facts, c, "-")) + ["ok"]
|     Err(e)  => [host] + map(columns, _ => "-") + [why(e)]
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| fn why(e) = match e:
|   {kind: 'timeout}   => "no answer in 20s"
|   {kind: 'exit, err} => trim(err)
|   _                  => e.message
| ;;
|
| # Rows of strings → aligned lines, two spaces between columns.
| fn table(rows):
|   widths = 0..len(rows[0]) |> map(i => rows |> map(r => len(r[i])) |> max)
|   rows |> map(fn(r):
|     n = len(r)
|     cells = zip(take(r, n - 1), widths) |> map(((c, w)) => pad_right(c, w))
|     join(cells + [last(r)], "  ")
|   ;;)
| ;;
|
| file = first(args) ?? fail("usage: inventory.fung hosts.txt")
| hosts = fs.read_lines(file)! |> filter(h => h != "")
| rows = conc.pmap(hosts, row, limit: 10)
| each(table([["host"] + columns + ["status"]] + rows), print)
| if any(rows, r => last(r) != "ok"): exit(1) ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual recipes/server-table.md:2
-- stdout
| fn parse_facts(text) = lines(text)
|   |> map(l => split(l, "="))
|   |> filter(kv => len(kv) >= 2)
|   |> fold({}, (m, [k, ...v]) => put(m, trim(k), trim(join(v, "="))))
|
| fn table(rows):
|   widths = 0..len(rows[0]) |> map(i => rows |> map(r => len(r[i])) |> max)
|   rows |> map(fn(r):
|     n = len(r)
|     cells = zip(take(r, n - 1), widths) |> map(((c, w)) => pad_right(c, w))
|     join(cells + [last(r)], "  ")
|   ;;)
| ;;
|
| facts = parse_facts("Welcome to web1!\nkernel=6.8.0\ncpus=4\nopts=a=b\n")
| print(facts)
| each(table([["host", "kernel", "cpus"], ["web1", facts.kernel, facts.cpus], ["db1", "-", "-"]]), print)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual recipes/ssh-collect.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Runs one command on many servers at once over ssh and prints every
| # output line after its host's name. Exit code 1 if any host failed.
| # usage: fung pssh.fung hosts.txt -- uptime
| import conc
| import fs
|
| # The arguments before "--" and after it.
| fn split_args(xs, before) = match xs:
|   []             => (before, [])
|   ["--", ...cmd] => (before, cmd)
|   [x, ...rest]   => split_args(rest, before + [x])
| ;;
|
| # Ok(output) of cmd on one host, or Err(why).
| fn run_on(host, cmd):
|   ssh = ["ssh", "-o", "BatchMode=yes", "-o", "ConnectTimeout=5", host]
|   match timeout(30s, () => exec(ssh + cmd)):
|     Ok(res)                       => Ok(res.out)
|     Err({kind: 'timeout})         => Err("no answer in 30s")
|     Err({kind: 'exit, code, err}) => Err("exit ${code}: ${trim(err)}")
|     Err(e)                        => Err(e.message)
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| usage = "usage: pssh.fung hosts.txt -- command..."
| (before, cmd) = split_args(args, [])
| file = first(before) ?? fail(usage)
| if is_empty(cmd): fail(usage) ;;
| hosts = fs.read_lines(file)! |> map(trim) |> filter(h => h != "" and not starts_with(h, "#"))
|
| results = conc.pmap(hosts, h => run_on(h, cmd), limit: 8)
| width = hosts |> map(len) |> max
| for (host, r) in zip(hosts, results):
|   match r:
|     Ok(out)  => each(lines(out), l => print(pad_right(host, width), "|", l))
|     Err(why) => eprint(pad_right(host, width), "! FAILED:", why)
|   ;;
| ;;
| if any(results, is_err): exit(1) ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual recipes/ssh-collect.md:2
-- stdout
| results = [Ok("up"), Err("refused"), Ok("up"), Err("timeout")]
| failed = results |> filter(is_err) |> len
| print("${failed} of ${len(results)} failed")
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual recipes/stdin-filter.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Reads an access log on standard input and passes on the requests that
| # failed (5xx) or were slow, one tab-separated line each:
| #   time  status  milliseconds  method  path
| # usage: tail -f access.log | fung slowreq.fung [--slow 1s] | sort ...
| import cli
| import regex
|
| # 10.0.0.7 - - [20/Oct/2026:09:00:02 +0000] "POST /api/orders HTTP/1.1" 502 0 3.004
| entry = `^\S+ \S+ \S+ \[([^\]]+)\] "(\S+) (\S+) [^"]*" (\d{3}) \d+ ([\d.]+)$`
|
| fn parse(line) = match regex.find(line, entry):
|   nil                                      => nil
|   {groups: [at, method, path, code, secs]} =>
|     {at, method, path, status: int(code), took: parse_float(secs)! * 1s}
| ;;
|
| # One line at a time until the input ends, so it works on `tail -f` too.
| # `seen` counts lines; returns (lines seen, lines kept, lines not understood).
| fn filter_lines(slow, seen, kept, bad):
|   line = read_line()!
|   if line == nil: return (seen, kept, bad) ;;
|   r = parse(line)
|   if r == nil: return filter_lines(slow, seen + 1, kept, bad + 1) ;;
|   if r.status < 500 and r.took < slow: return filter_lines(slow, seen + 1, kept, bad) ;;
|   print(join([r.at, str(r.status), str(r.took // 1ms), r.method, r.path], "\t"))
|   filter_lines(slow, seen + 1, kept + 1, bad)
| ;;
|
| opts = cli.parse(args)
| slow = parse_dur(get(opts.flags, "slow", "1s")) ?? fail("--slow takes a duration like 500ms")
| (seen, kept, bad) = filter_lines(slow, 0, 0, 0)
| eprint("kept ${kept} of ${seen} lines, ${bad} not understood")
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual recipes/unreachable-servers.md:1
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Checks that every server answers over ssh, trying each up to three
| # times. Prints nothing when all answer (quiet in cron); otherwise one
| # line per server that did not, and exit code 1. With ALERT_WEBHOOK set,
| # also posts the list there as JSON.
| # usage: fung reachable.fung hosts.txt
| import conc
| import fs
| import http
| import json
|
| # One try: Ok(nil) when the server ran `true`, else Err(why).
| fn try_once(host):
|   cmd = ["ssh", "-o", "BatchMode=yes", "-o", "ConnectTimeout=5", host, "true"]
|   match timeout(10s, () => exec(cmd)):
|     Ok(_)                   => Ok(nil)
|     Err({kind: 'timeout})   => Err("no answer in 10s")
|     Err({kind: 'exit, err}) => Err(trim(err))
|     Err(e)                  => Err(e.message)
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| fn check(host) = conc.retry(() => try_once(host), times: 3, delay: 2s)
|
| file = first(args) ?? fail("usage: reachable.fung hosts.txt")
| hosts = fs.read_lines(file)! |> filter(h => h != "")
| down = zip(hosts, conc.pmap(hosts, check, limit: 20))
|   |> filter(((_, r)) => is_err(r))
|   |> map(((host, Err(why))) => {host, why})
|
| if is_empty(down): exit(0) ;;
| for d in down:
|   print("DOWN", d.host, "-", d.why)
| ;;
| match env("ALERT_WEBHOOK"):
|   nil => nil
|   url =>
|     text = "${len(down)} of ${len(hosts)} servers down: ${down |> map(d => d.host) |> join(", ")}"
|     http.post(url, json.stringify({text}), headers: {"content-type": "application/json"})!
| ;;
| exit(1)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

| fn total_size(dir):
|   fs.list(dir)!
|     |> map(f => fs.stat("${dir}/${f}")!.size)
|     |> fold(0, (acc, n) => acc + n)
| ;;

| # 식 하나짜리
| fn add(a, b) = a + b
|
| # 블록 본문: 마지막 식이 반환값
| fn report(dir):
|   n = len(dir)
|   "${dir}: ${n}"
| ;;
|
| # 람다: 본문은 식 하나
| double = x => x * 2
| both = (a, b) => a + b
| later = () => print("run")
|
| # 익명 함수: 여러 문장이 필요할 때
| shout = fn(s):
|   t = upper(s)
|   t + "!"
| ;;
|
| print(add(1, 2), report("logs"), double(4), both(1, 2), shout("hi"))
| later()

==== manual reference/modules/conc.md:6
-- stdout
| import conc
|
| fn produce(s, name, n):
|   (tx, rx) = channel(1)
|   spawn(s, fn():
|     each(range(1, n + 1), i => send(tx, "${name}-${i}")!)
|     close(tx)
|   ;;)
|   rx
| ;;
|
| got = scope(fn(s):
|   merged = conc.fan_in(s, [produce(s, "web", 2), produce(s, "db", 1)])
|   for v in merged:
|     v
|   ;;
| ;;)
| print(sort(got))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

| ;;, times: 5, delay: 10ms)
| print(r)

==== manual reference/modules/http.md:3
-- stdout
| import http
|
| fn probe(url) = match http.get(url, timeout: 3s):
|   Ok(r) when r.status < 400 => "up (${r.status})"
|   Ok(r)                     => "error (${r.status})"
|   Err({kind: 'timeout})     => "timeout"
|   Err(e)                    => "down: ${e.message}"
| ;;
|
| print(probe("https://example.com"))
| print(probe("https://example.com/missing"))
| print(probe("http://127.0.0.1:1/"))
| print(probe("https://expired.badssl.com/"))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual reference/modules/http.md:5
-- stdout
| import http
| import json
|
| fn notify(text):
|   url = env("WEBHOOK_URL") ?? fail("WEBHOOK_URL is not set")
|   r = http.post(url, json.stringify({text}), headers: {"Content-Type": "application/json"}, timeout: 5s)?
|   r.status < 300 then Ok(nil) else Err({kind: 'http, message: "webhook answered ${r.status}"})
| ;;
|
| match notify("backup finished"):
|   Ok(_)  => print("sent")
|   Err(e) => eprint("notify failed:", e.message)
| ;;
-- stderr

| import http

==== manual reference/modules/http.md:9
-- stdout
| import http
| import json
|
| fn json_response(status, data) = {status, headers: {"Content-Type": "application/json"}, body: json.stringify(data)}
|
| hits = atom(0)
|
| fn handle(req):
|   n = swap(hits, x => x + 1)
|   match (req.method, req.path):
|     ("GET", "/stats") => json_response(200, {requests: n})
|     ("POST", "/echo") => match json.parse(req.body):
|       Ok(data) => json_response(200, {received: data, from: get(req.headers, "user-agent")})
|       Err(e)   => {status: 400, body: "bad json: ${e.message}"}
|     ;;
|     ("GET", "/hello") => "hello, ${get(req.query, "name", "stranger")}"
|     _                 => {status: 404, body: "not found: ${req.path}"}
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| http.serve("127.0.0.1:8080", handle, drain: 5s)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual reference/modules/http.md:10
-- stdout
| import http
|
| (tx, rx) = channel(1)
| r = scope(fn(s):
|   spawn(s, () => http.serve("127.0.0.1:0", req => "pong", ready: a => send(tx, a)!))
|   base = "http://${recv(rx)!}"
|   print(http.get("${base}/ping")!.body)
|   request_stop()
|   "stopped"
| ;;)
| print(r)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual reference/prelude/concurrency.md:1
-- stdout
| import time
|
| start = time.now()
| results = scope(fn(s):
|   tasks = for h in ["web-1", "web-2", "web-3"]:
|     spawn(s, fn():
|       sleep(300ms)
|       "${h} ok"
|     ;;)
|   ;;
|   map(tasks, await)
| ;;)
| print(results)
| print("faster than one by one:", time.now() - start < 900ms)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual reference/prelude/concurrency.md:2

| print(r)
| match r:
|   Err({kind: 'timeout}) => print("slow")
|   _                     => print("done")
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual reference/prelude/concurrency.md:3

|   a = spawn(s, () => 6 * 7)
|   b = spawn(s, () => 1 + 1)
|   [await(a), await(b)]
| ;;)
| print(r)
-- stderr

==== manual reference/prelude/concurrency.md:4

|   spawn(s, fn():
|     sleep(50ms)
|     print("slow task done")
|   ;;)
|   print("body done")

| print("scope returned")
-- stderr

==== manual reference/prelude/concurrency.md:5

| scope(fn(s):
|   spawn(s, fn():
|     sleep(10ms)
|     fail("worker died")
|   ;;)
|   spawn(s, fn():

|     print("never printed")
|   ;;)
| ;;)
| print("not reached")
-- stderr

==== manual reference/prelude/concurrency.md:7
-- stdout
| supervise(fn(s):
|   spawn(s, () => fail("boom"))
|   spawn(s, () => print("still runs"))
| ;;, e => eprint("handled: ${e.message}"))

-- exit: 0
==== manual reference/prelude/concurrency.md:8

|   t = spawn(s, () => 1 / 0)
|   await(t)
| ;;, e => print("on_fail:", e.code, e.message))
| print(r)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual reference/prelude/concurrency.md:9
-- stdout
| hosts = ["web-1", "web-2", "web-3"]
| r = supervise(fn(s):
|   tasks = for h in hosts:
|     spawn(s, () => h == "web-2" then fail("${h} unreachable") else "${h} ok")
|   ;;
|   map(tasks, await)
| ;;, _ => nil)
| print(r |> filter(x => not is_err(x)))
| print(r |> filter(is_err) |> len, "failed")
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual reference/prelude/concurrency.md:19
-- stdout
| winner = scope(fn(s):
|   (tx, rx) = channel(2)
|   a = spawn(s, fn():
|     sleep(10ms)
|     send(tx, "mirror-a")!
|   ;;)
|   b = spawn(s, fn():
|     sleep(1s)
|     send(tx, "mirror-b")!
|   ;;)
|   first = recv(rx)!
|   cancel(a)
|   cancel(b)
|   first
| ;;)
| print(winner)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual reference/prelude/concurrency.md:21
-- stdout
| fn numbers(s, n):
|   (tx, rx) = channel()
|   spawn(s, () => each(0..n, i => send(tx, i)))
|   rx
| ;;
|
| scope(fn(s):
|   for x in numbers(s, 3):
|     print(x)
|   ;;
| ;;)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual reference/prelude/concurrency.md:22
-- stdout
| out = scope(fn(s):
|   (jobs_tx, jobs) = channel()
|   (res_tx, results) = channel()
|   for _ in 1..=2:
|     spawn(s, fn():
|       for j in jobs:
|         send(res_tx, j * j)!
|       ;;
|     ;;)
|   ;;
|   spawn(s, () => each(1..=4, j => send(jobs_tx, j)))
|   results |> take(4) |> sort
| ;;)
| print(out)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual reference/prelude/process.md:2
-- stdout
| fn run(cmd) = match exec(cmd):
|   Ok(r)                    => "ok: ${trim(r.out)}"
|   Err({kind: 'exit, code}) => "exited with ${code}"
|   Err({kind: 'not_found})  => "no such command"
| ;;
|
| print(run(["echo", "fine"]))
| print(run(["sh", "-c", "exit 3"]))
| print(run(["no-such-command-xyz"]))

==== manual reference/prelude/results.md:2
-- stdout
| fn port_of(s):
|   n = parse_int(s)?
|   n > 0 and n < 65536 then Ok(n) else Err({kind: 'invalid, message: "out of range: ${n}"})
| ;;
|
| print(port_of("8080"), port_of("x") ?? 80)
| match port_of("70000"):
|   Ok(p)  => print("port", p)
|   Err(e) => print("bad:", e.message)
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual reference/prelude/results.md:3
-- stdout
| print(is_ok(Ok(1)), is_ok(Err(1)), is_ok(nil), is_ok(42))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

| fn label(n):

| ;;

==== manual vm/06-resolve.md:13
-- stdout
| fn describe(host, port):
|   addr = "${host}:${port}"
|   msg = if port == 443:
|     tls = "https"
|     "${tls}://${addr}"
|   else:
|     "http://${addr}"
|   ;;
|   msg
| ;;
| print(describe("db1", 443))

|   map(fs, f => f())

|

| ;;

==== manual vm/10-values-and-memory.md:5
-- stdout
| n = 20000
| counts = fold(0..n, {}, (acc, i) => put(acc, "k${i % 5000}", get(acc, "k${i % 5000}", 0) + 1))
| items = fold(0..n, [], (acc, i) => acc + [i])
|
| print(len(counts), counts["k42"])
| print(len(items), items[19999])
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual vm/10-values-and-memory.md:7
-- stdout
| fn numbers(n) = scope(fn(s):
|   (tx, rx) = channel()
|   spawn(s, () => each(0..n, i => send(tx, i)!))
|   fold(rx, 0, (acc, x) => acc + x)
| ;;)
|
| print(numbers(5))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual vm/10-values-and-memory.md:9
-- stdout
| fn drop_it(_x) = nil
|
| fn closes_early() = scope(fn(s):
|   (tx, rx) = channel()
|   [drop_it(tx), recv(rx)]
| ;;)
|
| print(closes_early())
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== manual vm/10-values-and-memory.md:10
-- stdout
| levels = ["INFO", "WARN", "INFO", "ERROR", "INFO"]
| entries = map(0..30000, i => levels[i % 5])
|
| counts = fold(entries, {}, (acc, lv) => put(acc, lv, get(acc, lv, 0) + 1))
| newest_first = reverse(fold(0..30000, [], (acc, i) => acc + [i]))
|
| print(counts)
| print(take(newest_first, 3))
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== manual vm/12-scheduler.md:2
-- stdout
| n = 20000
| (tx, rx) = channel()
|
| total = scope(fn(s):
|   each(0..n, i => spawn(s, () => recv(rx)))
|   each(0..n, i => send(tx, i)!)
|   n
| ;;)
| print(total, "tasks waited and finished")

|   request_stop()
| ;;)

==== crates/fung-cli/tests/data/test_ends/top_task_exit.fung
-- stdout
| # A task in a top-level scope asks for exit(3): no block runs, and the
| # top level fails. Ordered by state: the task can only send on `c_tx`
| # after taking `go`'s value, and it asks for the exit on the same turn
| # of the run thread.
| (go_tx, go) = channel(1)
| (c_tx, c) = channel()
| scope(fn(s):
|   spawn(s, fn():
|     recv(go)
|     send(c_tx, 1)
|     exit(3)
|   ;;)
|   send(go_tx, 1)
|   recv(c)
| ;;)
|
| test "one": assert true ;;
| test "two": assert true ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== examples/api.fung
-- stdout
| # api: the handlers behind serve.fung — GET /health, POST /echo (echoes
| # the JSON body back with a timestamp), anything else 404.
| import http
| import json
| import time
|
| fn json_response(status, data) =
|   {status, headers: {"content-type": "application/json"}, body: json.stringify(data)}
|
| fn health(started, requests) =
|   json_response(200, {ok: true, uptime: str(time.now() - started), requests: deref(requests)})
|
| fn echo(req):
|   match json.parse(req.body):
|     Ok(data) => json_response(200, {received: data, at: time.format(time.now())})
|     Err(e)   => {status: 400, body: "bad json: ${e.message}"}
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| # Builds the handler: the request count lives in an atom the closure
| # holds (05: shared state is an atom, updated by a pure function), so
| # the handler's tasks each add their request with one swap.
| fn handler(started, requests):
|   fn(req):
|     swap(requests, n => n + 1)
|     match (req.method, req.path):
|       ("GET", "/health") => health(started, requests)
|       ("POST", "/echo")  => echo(req)
|       _                  => {status: 404, body: "not found: ${req.path}"}
|     ;;
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| test "health, echo and 404":
|   scope(fn(app):
|     (port_tx, port) = channel(1)
|     h = handler(time.now(), atom(0))
|     s = spawn(app, () => http.serve("127.0.0.1:0", h, ready: a => send(port_tx, a)))
|     base = "http://" + recv(port)!
|     r = http.get("${base}/health")!
|     assert r.status == 200
|     assert json.parse(r.body)!.ok == true
|     e = http.post("${base}/echo", json.stringify({x: 1}))!
|     assert json.parse(e.body)!.received.x == 1
|     assert http.post("${base}/echo", "{")!.status == 400
|     assert http.get("${base}/nope")!.status == 404
|     assert json.parse(http.get("${base}/health")!.body)!.requests == 5
|     cancel(s)
|   ;;)
| ;;

==== examples/bigfiles.fung
-- stdout
| # bigfiles: find the largest files under a directory.
| import fs
| import path
|
| # Files below dir as (path, size), largest first, keeping at most n.
| fn largest(dir, n):
|   files = fs.walk(dir)!
|   sized = files |> map(p => (p, get(fs.stat(p) ?? {}, "size", 0)))
|   sized |> sort_by(e => match e: (_, s) => -s ;;) |> take(n)
| ;;
|
| # Bytes as a short human string: 1536 → "1.5K".
| fn human(n):
|   n < 1024 then "${n}B"
|     else n < 1024 * 1024 then "${n * 10 // 1024 / 10.0}K"
|     else "${n * 10 // (1024 * 1024) / 10.0}M"
| ;;
|
| test "human sizes":
|   assert human(10) == "10B"
|   assert human(1536) == "1.5K"
|   assert human(3 * 1024 * 1024) == "3.0M"
| ;;
|
| test "largest keeps order and count":
|   fs.with_temp_dir(fn(d):
|     fs.write(path.join(d, "a"), repeat("x", 10))!
|     fs.write(path.join(d, "b"), repeat("x", 30))!
|     fs.write(path.join(d, "c"), repeat("x", 20))!
|     got = largest(d, 2) |> map(e => match e: (p, s) => (path.base(p), s) ;;)
|     assert got == [("b", 30), ("c", 20)]
|   ;;)
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== examples/dotenv.fung
-- stdout
| # dotenv: read KEY=VALUE files and check them against requirements.
|
| # Text → map of KEY → VALUE. Blank lines and # comments are skipped;
| # a `export ` prefix and surrounding quotes are removed.
| fn parse(text):
|   rows = lines(text) |> map(trim) |> filter(l => l != "" and not starts_with(l, "#"))
|   fold(rows, {}, fn(acc, line):
|     (k, v) = match split(line, "="):
|       [k, ...rest] => (trim(replace(k, "export ", "")), trim(join(rest, "=")))
|     ;;
|     put(acc, k, trim(trim(v, "\""), "'"))
|   ;;)
| ;;
|
| # Problems with a config: missing or empty required keys, bad ports.
| fn problems(cfg, required, ports):
|   missing = filter(required, k => get(cfg, k, "") == "") |> map(k => "missing: ${k}")
|   bad_ports = filter(ports, k => contains(cfg, k) and not valid_port(get(cfg, k)))
|     |> map(k => "not a port: ${k}=${get(cfg, k)}")
|   missing + bad_ports
| ;;
|
| fn valid_port(s) = match parse_int(s):
|   Ok(n) => n > 0 and n < 65536
|   _     => false
| ;;
|
| test "parse handles comments, export and quotes":
|   cfg = parse("# db\nexport DB_HOST=\"localhost\"\nDB_PORT=5432\n\nTOKEN='a=b=c'\n")
|   assert cfg == {"DB_HOST": "localhost", "DB_PORT": "5432", "TOKEN": "a=b=c"}
| ;;
|
| test "problems reports missing keys and bad ports":
|   cfg = parse("DB_HOST=x\nDB_PORT=99999\nEMPTY=\n")
|   assert problems(cfg, ["DB_HOST", "EMPTY", "SECRET"], ["DB_PORT"]) == ["missing: EMPTY", "missing: SECRET", "not a port: DB_PORT=99999"]
|   assert problems(parse("DB_PORT=80"), [], ["DB_PORT"]) == []
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== examples/envcheck.fung

| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Checks a .env file: required keys present and non-empty, ports valid.
| # usage: fung examples/envcheck.fung <file> KEY... [--ports A,B]
| import fs
| import cli
| import "./dotenv" (parse, problems)
|
| opts = cli.parse(args)
| file = first(opts.rest) ?? fail("usage: envcheck.fung <file> KEY... [--ports A,B]")
| required = drop(opts.rest, 1)
| ports = split(get(opts.flags, "ports", ""), ",") |> filter(p => p != "")
|

| if not is_empty(found): exit(1) ;;

==== examples/kebab.fung
-- stdout
| # kebab: file-name normalisation used by rename.fung.
| import path
|
| # Lowercase, keep letters and digits, turn everything else into "-",
| # squeeze repeats, trim the ends.
| fn kebab(name):
|   kept = for c in chars(lower(name)): c in "abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789" then c else "-" ;;
|   join(kept) |> split("-") |> filter(p => p != "") |> join("-")
| ;;
|
| # The new name for one entry; names that would vanish (no ASCII letters at
| # all) are kept as they are.
| fn target(file):
|   stem = kebab(path.stem(file))
|   stem == "" then file else stem + lower(path.ext(file))
| ;;
|
| test "kebab squeezes and trims":
|   assert kebab("My Report (final)") == "my-report-final"
|   assert kebab("--a--b--") == "a-b"
|   assert kebab("참치") == ""
| ;;
|
| test "target keeps names without ascii letters":
|   assert target("My File (1).TXT") == "my-file-1.txt"
|   assert target("참치.md") == "참치.md"
|   assert target("already-ok.txt") == "already-ok.txt"
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== examples/loglines.fung
-- stdout
| # loglines: parse "2026-10-13 12:00:01 LEVEL message" lines and summarise them.
| # Used by logsum.fung; the tests document the format.
|
| # One line → {level, message} or nil for lines that do not fit.
| fn parse(line):
|   parts = split(trim(line), " ")
|   match parts:
|     [_, _, level, ...rest] when level in ["INFO", "WARN", "ERROR"] => {level: level, message: join(rest, " ")}
|     _                                                              => nil
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| # Lines → parsed entries, skipping what does not parse.
| fn entries(xs) = xs |> map(parse) |> filter(e => e != nil)
|
| # Count of entries per level, every level present (zero when absent).
| fn by_level(es):
|   counts = group_by(es, e => e.level)
|   for level in ["INFO", "WARN", "ERROR"]: (level, len(get(counts, level, []))) ;;
| ;;
|
| # The n most frequent messages of one level, as (message, count), most first.
| fn top_messages(es, level, n):
|   same = filter(es, e => e.level == level)
|   groups = group_by(same, e => e.message)
|   pairs(groups) |> map(p => match p: (m, xs) => (m, len(xs)) ;;) |> sort_by(p => match p: (_, c) => -c ;;) |> take(n)
| ;;
|
| test "parse keeps the message words together":
|   assert parse("2026-10-13 12:00:01 ERROR disk full on /var") == {level: "ERROR", message: "disk full on /var"}
|   assert parse("garbage") == nil
|   assert parse("2026-10-13 12:00:01 DEBUG x") == nil
| ;;
|
| test "counts and top messages":
|   es = entries(["t d ERROR a", "t d ERROR b", "t d ERROR a", "t d WARN w", "noise"])
|   assert by_level(es) == [("INFO", 0), ("WARN", 1), ("ERROR", 3)]
|   assert top_messages(es, "ERROR", 1) == [("a", 2)]
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== examples/logsum.fung
-- stdout
| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Summarises a log file: lines per level and the most frequent errors.
| # usage: fung examples/logsum.fung <file> [top]      (or: cat log | fung examples/logsum.fung -)
| import fs
| import "./loglines" (entries, by_level, top_messages)
|
| fn read_input(source) = source == "-" then read_lines()! else fs.read_lines(source)!
|
| fn report(lines, top):
|   es = entries(lines)
|   print("lines:", len(lines), " parsed:", len(es))
|   each(by_level(es), fn(pair):
|     (level, count) = pair
|     print(pad_right(level, 6), count)
|   ;;)
|   errors = top_messages(es, "ERROR", top)
|   if not is_empty(errors):
|     print("top errors:")
|     each(errors, fn(pair):
|       (message, count) = pair
|       print(" ", pad_left(str(count), 4), message)
|     ;;)
|   ;;
|   nil
| ;;
|
| source = first(args) ?? fail("usage: logsum.fung <file|-> [top]")
| top = len(args) > 1 then int(args[1]) else 3
| report(read_input(source), top)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

| #!/usr/bin/env fung
| # Renames every file in a directory to kebab-case:

|   ;;)
| ;;

==== examples/urlcheck.fung
-- stdout
| # urlcheck: fetches one URL and summarises the answer — used by
| # checkurls.fung in parallel.
| import http
| import json
| import regex
| import time
|
| # The top-level keys of a JSON body, [] for anything else.
| fn json_keys(resp):
|   ct = get(resp.headers, "content-type", "")
|   if not regex.is_match(ct, "^application/json"): return [] ;;
|   match json.parse(resp.body):
|     Ok(m) when type_of(m) == 'map => keys(m)
|     _                             => []
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| fn check(url, limit = 5s):
|   t0 = time.now()
|   r = http.get(url, timeout: limit)
|   took = time.now() - t0
|   match r:
|     Ok(resp) => {url, ok: resp.status < 400, status: resp.status, took, keys: json_keys(resp)}
|     Err(e)   => {url, ok: false, status: e.kind, took, keys: []}
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| # Prints one line per result and reports whether all were ok.
| fn report(results):
|   each(results, r => print(r.ok then "ok  " else "FAIL", r.status, r.took, r.url, join(r.keys, ",")))
|   all(results, r => r.ok)
| ;;
|
| test "check reports status and json keys":
|   scope(fn(app):
|     (port_tx, port) = channel(1)
|     handler = fn(req):
|       req.path == "/j" then {headers: {"content-type": "application/json"}, body: `{"a":1,"b":2}`} else {status: 500, body: "x"}
|     ;;
|     s = spawn(app, () => http.serve("127.0.0.1:0", handler, ready: a => send(port_tx, a)))
|     base = "http://" + recv(port)!
|     good = check("${base}/j", 2s)
|     assert good.ok
|     assert good.keys == ["a", "b"]
|     bad = check("${base}/x", 2s)
|     assert not bad.ok
|     assert bad.status == 500
|     down = check("http://127.0.0.1:1/", 2s)
|     assert down.status == 'network
|     cancel(s)
|   ;;)
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== extras/cli.fung
-- stdout
| # cli: command-line argument parsing (06). Small on purpose: flags and the
| # rest; checking and converting values is ordinary code.
| #
| #   cli.parse(["in", "--out", "x", "--", "-v"])
| #   → {flags: {out: "x"}, rest: ["in", "-v"]}
| #
| # `--name value` and `--name=value` set a flag; `--name` alone, or a name
| # listed in `bools`, is true. Flag names turn `-` into `_` (`dry_run`).
| # Arguments not starting with `--` go to rest in order; everything after
| # a bare `--` goes to rest. A lone `-` is an ordinary argument.
| fn parse(argv, bools = []) = _parse(argv, bools, {flags: {}, rest: []})
|
| fn _parse(argv, bools, acc) = match argv:
|   []                                     => acc
|   ["--", ...rest]                        => put(acc, "rest", acc.rest + rest)
|   [a, ...rest] when starts_with(a, "--") =>
|     (k, v, left) = _flag(a[2..], rest, bools)
|     _parse(left, bools, put(acc, "flags", put(acc.flags, k, v)))
|   [a, ...rest]                           => _parse(rest, bools, put(acc, "rest", acc.rest + [a]))
| ;;
|
| # One flag body (after `--`) with the remaining arguments → (key, value, rest).
| fn _flag(body, rest, bools) = match split(body, "="):
|   [name, ...vals] when not is_empty(vals) => (_key(name), join(vals, "="), rest)
|   _                                       => body in bools then (_key(body), true, rest)
|     else match rest:
|       [v, ...more] when not starts_with(v, "--") => (_key(body), v, more)
|       _                                          => (_key(body), true, rest)
|     ;;
| ;;
|
| fn _key(name) = replace(name, "-", "_")
|
| test "flags, values, and rest":
|   assert parse(["a", "--trash", "dir", "b"]) == {flags: {trash: "dir"}, rest: ["a", "b"]}
|   assert parse(["--dry-run", "x"], bools: ["dry-run"]) == {flags: {dry_run: true}, rest: ["x"]}
|   assert parse(["--n=3", "--", "--not-a-flag", "-"]) == {flags: {n: "3"}, rest: ["--not-a-flag", "-"]}
|   assert parse(["--v"]) == {flags: {v: true}, rest: []}
|   assert parse(["--a", "--b", "1"]) == {flags: {a: true, b: "1"}, rest: []}
|   assert parse([]) == {flags: {}, rest: []}
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== extras/fs.fung
-- stdout
| # fs: files and directories (06). The primitives live in _native.fs; this
| # file decides the public names and adds the fung-level wrappers.
| import _native.fs as _n
|
| read = _n.read
| write = _n.write
| append = _n.append
| list = _n.list
| glob = _n.glob
| walk = _n.walk
| exists = _n.exists
| stat = _n.stat
| mkdir = _n.mkdir
| remove = _n.remove
| remove_all = _n.remove_all
| move = _n.move
| copy = _n.copy
| open = _n.open
| close = _n.close
| write_line = _n.write_line
|
| # Reads a whole file as a list of lines (the same rule as read_lines).
| fn read_lines(path) = Ok(lines(_n.read(path)?))
|
| # Opens path, calls f with the handle, and always closes it (03: a
| # `with`). f's result — a value or the Err that `?` returned — is passed
| # through.
| #
| # The handle is opened inside the `with` (05), so nothing can come between
| # opening and the promise to close it. A close that fails (a write that
| # could not be flushed) is a runtime error; a handle f closed itself is
| # not a failure.
| fn with_open(path, f, mode = 'read, perm = nil) = with(() => _n.open(path, mode, perm), r => _with_ok(r, f), r => _close_opened(r))
|
| # Creates a temporary directory, calls f with its path, and removes it,
| # whatever f does (a `with`, made inside it as `with_open` opens). A
| # removal that fails is a runtime error.
| fn with_temp_dir(f) = with(() => _n.temp_dir(), r => _with_ok(r, f), r => _remove_made(r))
|
| # f of what was acquired, or the Err acquiring gave.
| fn _with_ok(r, f) = match r:
|   Ok(x) => f(x)
|   e     => e
| ;;
|
| fn _close_opened(r) = match r:
|   Ok(h) => match _n.close(h):
|     Err(e) when e.kind == 'closed => nil
|     c                             => c!
|   ;;
|   _     => nil
| ;;
|
| fn _remove_made(r) = match r:
|   Ok(d) => _n.remove_all(d)!
|   _     => nil
| ;;
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== tests/lang/assert_pass.fung

==== tests/lang/atoms.fung
-- stdout
| # An atom holds one value; swap is compare-and-set with a pure function.
| a = atom(1)
| print(swap(a, x => x + 1), deref(a), reset(a, 10), deref(a), a, type_of(a))
| # expect: 2 2 10 10 <atom> 'atom
|
| # Many tasks swap one atom: every update lands once.
| counter = atom(0)
| scope(s => each(for _ in 0..8: spawn(s, () => each(0..50, _ => swap(counter, n => n + 1))) ;;, await))
| print(deref(counter))
| # expect: 400
|
| # f may read (here the atom itself); an atom equals only itself.
| b = atom(1)
| print(swap(b, x => x + deref(b)), b == b, b == atom(1), swap(b, x => x + len([b])))
| # expect: 2 true false 3
|
| # A failing f leaves the value as it was.
| t = supervise(s => spawn(s, () => swap(b, x => x / 0)), _ => nil)
| print(await(t) |> is_err, deref(b))
| # expect: true 3
|
| # Temporaries and handles passed around.
| print(deref(atom(5)), swap(atom(5), x => x + 1))
| # expect: 5 6
|
| fn count(xs):
|   c = atom(0)
|   each(xs, x => swap(c, n => n + x))
|   deref(c)
| ;;
| fn mk() = atom(7)
| fn bump(c) = swap(c, x => x + 1)
| fn peek(c: atom) = deref(c)
| print(count([1, 2, 3]), deref(mk()), swap(mk(), x => x + 1), bump(atom(1)), peek(b))
| # expect: 6 7 8 2 3
|
| with(() => nil, _ => nil, _ => print("released", deref(counter)))
| # expect: released 400
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/cancel.fung
-- stdout
| scope(fn(s):
|   t = spawn(s, () => sleep(10s))
|   cancel(t)
|   u = spawn(s, () => sleep(10s))
|   # `_tx` keeps the channel open, so the receive waits
|   (_tx, rx) = channel()
|   print(await(t), timeout(10ms, () => await(u)), timeout(5ms, () => recv(rx)))
|   cancel(u)
| ;;)
| # expect: Err({kind: 'cancelled, message: "cancelled"}) Err({kind: 'timeout, message: "timed out after 10ms"}) Err({kind: 'timeout, message: "timed out after 5ms"})
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/channel_stream.fung
-- stdout
| # Iterating a channel calls the function as each value arrives.
| (tx, rx) = channel()
| (ack_tx, ack) = channel()
| fn produce(tx, ack):
|   for x in 1..=3:
|     send(tx, x)
|     recv(ack)
|   ;;
|   close(tx)
| ;;
| scope(fn(s):
|   spawn(s, () => produce(tx, ack))
|   for x in rx:
|     print("got ${x}")
|     send(ack_tx, nil)
|   ;;
| ;;)
| print("done")
| # expect: got 1
| # expect: got 2
| # expect: got 3
| # expect: done
|
| # Functions that stop early leave the rest in the channel.
| (b_tx, b) = channel(3)
| send(b_tx, 1); send(b_tx, 2); send(b_tx, 3); close(b_tx)
| print(find(b, x => x == 2), recv(b))
| # expect: 2 Ok(3)
| (c_tx, c) = channel(4)
| send(c_tx, 1); send(c_tx, 2); send(c_tx, 3); send(c_tx, 4); close(c_tx)
| print(take(c, 2), first(c), recv(c), first(c))
| # expect: [1, 2] 3 Ok(4) nil
|
| # Argument errors come before any value is received.
| (d_tx, d) = channel(2)
| send(d_tx, 1); send(d_tx, 2); close(d_tx)
| t = supervise(s => spawn(s, () => map(d, 3)), _ => nil)
| print(type_of(await(t)), recv(d))
| # expect: 'Err Ok(1)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/channels.fung
-- stdout
| (tx, rx) = channel()
| scope(fn(s):
|   spawn(s, fn(): each(1..=5, x => send(tx, x)); close(tx) ;;)
|   print(fold(rx, 0, (a, x) => a + x))
| ;;)
| # expect: 15
| (buf_tx, buf) = channel(2)
| send(buf_tx, "a")
| close(buf_tx)
| print(recv(buf), recv(buf), send(buf_tx, "b"))
| # expect: Ok("a") Err({kind: 'closed, message: "channel closed"}) Err({kind: 'closed, message: "channel closed"})
| print(tx, rx)
| # expect: <sender> <receiver>
|
| # A producer: only the task holds the sending end, so the channel
| # closes when the task ends and the loop below ends with it.
| fn numbers(s, n):
|   (tx, rx) = channel()
|   spawn(s, () => each(0..n, i => send(tx, i)))
|   rx
| ;;
|
| scope(fn(s):
|   for x in numbers(s, 3):
|     print(x)
|   ;;
| ;;)
| # expect: 0
| # expect: 1
| # expect: 2
| print("after the producer")
| # expect: after the producer
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== tests/lang/conc_helpers.fung
-- stdout
| # fan_in, pmap and retry live in the module `conc`.
| import conc
|
| (a_tx, a) = channel(2)
| (b_tx, b) = channel(2)
| each([1, 2], x => send(a_tx, x))
| close(a_tx)
| send(b_tx, 3)
| close(b_tx)
| print(scope(s => conc.fan_in(s, [a, b]) |> map(x => x) |> sort), conc.pmap([1, 2, 3], x => x * x, limit: 2), conc.pmap([], x => x))
| # expect: [1, 2, 3] [1, 4, 9] []
| print(conc.pmap([1, 2], x => x == 1 then Err('no) else x))
| # expect: [Err('no), 2]
| # `limit` is how many run at once, not a batch size: while item 0 waits
| # for item 2, the other worker goes on to items 1 and 2.
| (go_tx, go) = channel(1)
| waits = fn(i):
|   if i == 0: _ = recv(go) ;;
|   if i == 2: _ = send(go_tx, 1) ;;
|   i * 10
| ;;
| print(conc.pmap([0, 1, 2, 3], waits, limit: 2))
| # expect: [0, 10, 20, 30]
| print(conc.retry(() => Err("never"), times: 2))
| # expect: Err("never")
|
| # fan_in over two producers ends on its own: each producer's channel
| # closes when its task ends, and then the merged one closes.
| fn numbers(s, from, n):
|   (tx, rx) = channel()
|   spawn(s, () => each(from..from + n, i => send(tx, i)))
|   rx
| ;;
|
| print(scope(s => conc.fan_in(s, [numbers(s, 0, 2), numbers(s, 10, 2)]) |> map(x => x) |> sort))
| # expect: [0, 1, 10, 11]
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== tests/lang/discard.fung
-- stdout
| # What a discarded Err (02) leaves alone.
|
| # `_ = …` discards on purpose.
| _ = Err("ignored")
| _ = exec(["false"])
|
| # A value place keeps the Err: an assignment, an argument, a fn's result.
| r = Err("kept")
| print(r)
| # expect: Err("kept")
| fn fails() = Err("returned")
| print(fails())
| # expect: Err("returned")
| print(is_err(exec(["false"])))
| # expect: true
|
| # A for in a value place still builds its list, Errs and all.
| xs = for x in [1, 2]: x == 2 then Err(x) else Ok(x) ;;
| print(xs)
| # expect: [Ok(1), Err(2)]
|
| # A nested for statement in a for statement is discarded as well.
| for x in [1, 2]:
|   for y in [x]:
|     print(y)
|   ;;
| ;;
| # expect: 1
| # expect: 2
|
| # Ok and other values are discarded as before.
| Ok(1)
| nil
| [Err(1)]
| each([1], x => Ok(x))
| print("done")
| # expect: done
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/discard_callbacks.fung
-- stdout
| # Callbacks whose results are used keep their Errs.
| print(with(() => 1, _ => Err("use"), _ => nil))
| # expect: Err("use")
| print(map([1], x => Err(x)))
| # expect: [Err(1)]
|
| # A use that fails wins over a release that returns Err; the release's
| # error is a note (03).
| r = supervise(fn(s):
|   t = spawn(s, () => with(() => 1, _ => fail("in use"), _ => Err("in release")))
|   await(t)
| ;;, e => nil)
| print(r)
| # expect: Err({kind: 'error, code: "E0803", message: "in use", at: "tests/lang/discard_callbacks.fung:10"})
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/dur_numbers.fung

==== tests/lang/http_serve.fung
-- stdout
| # A server that counts its requests with an atom.
| import http
| import json
|
| hits = atom(0)
| handle = fn(req):
|   swap(hits, n => n + 1)
|   match req.path:
|     "/health" => {status: 200, headers: {"content-type": "application/json"}, body: json.stringify({ok: true, n: deref(hits)})}
|     "/echo"   => "${req.method} ${req.body} ${get(req.query, "x", "-")}"
|     _         => {status: 404, body: "no such page"}
|   ;;
| ;;
|
| # The server runs in a child of the scope; cancelling it lets the scope end.
| server = scope(fn(s):
|   (port_tx, port) = channel(1)
|   server = spawn(s, () => http.serve("127.0.0.1:0", handle, ready: addr => send(port_tx, addr)))
|   base = "http://" + recv(port)!
|
|   r = http.get("${base}/health")!
|   print(r.status, r.headers["content-type"], json.parse(r.body)!.ok)
|   r = http.post("${base}/echo?x=1", "hello")!
|   print(r.status, r.body, r.headers["content-type"])
|   r = http.get("${base}/nowhere")!
|   print(r.status, r.body)
|   print(deref(hits))
|   cancel(server)
|   server
| ;;)
| # expect: 200 application/json true
| # expect: 200 POST hello 1 text/plain; charset=utf-8
| # expect: 404 no such page
| # expect: 3
| print(await(server))
| # expect: Err({kind: 'cancelled, message: "cancelled"})
|
| # A clean stop (here a stop request as soon as it listens) is Ok(nil),
| # so `!` on serve stops only on a failure to listen.
| print(http.serve("127.0.0.1:0", req => "hi", ready: _ => request_stop()))
| # expect: Ok(nil)
| http.serve("127.0.0.1:0", req => "hi", ready: _ => request_stop())!
| print("after")
| # expect: after
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/ident_unicode.fung
-- stdout
| # Names follow UAX #31: combining marks may continue a name.
| नमस्ते = 1
| café = 2
| print(नमस्ते + café)
| # expect: 3
| print(Été, type_of(Été))
| # expect: 'Été 'sym
| café = 5
| print(café)
| # expect: 5
| # Names are normalized (NFC): the composed and decomposed spellings are one name.
| café = 6
| print(café, sym("café") == 'café)
| # expect: 6 true
| print('café == 'café, 'café == sym("café"))
| # expect: true true

==== tests/lang/local_lifetime.fung
-- stdout
| # A local's last read hands its value over, and a local is let go after
| # the statement that reads it last (03), so a channel a function made
| # closes when its producer is done.
| fn tens() = scope(fn(s):
|   (tx, rx) = channel()
|   spawn(s, () => each(0..3, i => send(tx, i)))
|   for x in rx: x * 10 ;;
| ;;)
| print(tens())
| # expect: [0, 10, 20]
|
| # Read last inside a branch: let go after the statement holding it.
| fn pick(flag) = scope(fn(s):
|   (tx, rx) = channel(3)
|   if flag:
|     spawn(s, () => send(tx, 1))
|   else:
|     spawn(s, () => send(tx, 2))
|   ;;
|   for x in rx: x ;;
| ;;)
| print(pick(true), pick(false))
| # expect: [1] [2]
|
| # The last read hands the value over within its statement: the sending
| # end moves into `drop_it`, which lets it go, so the channel is closed
| # before `recv` waits.
| fn drop_it(_x) = nil
| fn closes_early() = scope(fn(s):
|   (tx, rx) = channel()
|   [drop_it(tx), recv(rx)]
| ;;)
| print(closes_early())

==== tests/lang/match.fung

| fn describe(v) = match v:
|   0            => "zero"
|   1 | 2        => "small"
|   "s"          => "string"
|   'ok          => "ok symbol"
|   Ok(x)        => "ok ${x}"
|   (x, 0)       => "x-axis ${x}"
|   []           => "empty"
|   [first, ...] => "list from ${first}"
|   {name: n}    => "named ${n}"
|   n when n < 0 => "negative"
|   _            => "other"
| ;;
| print(describe(0), describe(-5), describe(2), describe("s"), describe('ok))
| # expect: zero negative small string ok symbol
| print(describe(Ok(9)), describe((4, 0)), describe([]), describe([7, 8]), describe({name: "z"}), describe(3.5))
| # expect: ok 9 x-axis 4 empty list from 7 named z other

|   []           => 0

| ;;

| # expect: 3

==== tests/lang/nfc_keys.fung
-- stdout
| # A key is a string as written: a name in a key place is not made NFC
| # (01). Here e + U+0301 (decomposed) and U+00E9 (composed) both show as e-acute.
| m = {"café": 1}
| print(m["café"], m.café)
| # expect: 1 1
| print({café: 1} == {"café": 1}, {café: 1} == {"café": 1})
| # expect: true false
|
| # What print shows reads back as the same key.
| print({"é": 1})
| # expect: {é: 1}
| print({é: 1} == {"é": 1})
| # expect: true
|
| # A shorthand: the key as written, the variable's name in NFC.
| café = 5
| k = {café}
| print(k["café"], café, get(k, "café", "none"))
| # expect: 5 5 none
| {café: v} = k
| print(v)
| # expect: 5
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== tests/lang/open_if.fung
-- stdout
| # Where an if without else is fine: nobody uses its value (01).
| fn shout(s):
|   if s != "":
|     print(s + "!")
|   ;;
| ;;
| shout("hi")
| # expect: hi!
| if false: print("no") ;;
| for x in [1, 2]:
|   if x > 1: print(x) ;;
| ;;
| # expect: 2
| each([3], fn(x): if x > 0: print(x) ;; ;;)
| # expect: 3
|
| # A value place says what it means with else.
| x = if false: 1 else: nil ;;
| print(x)
| # expect: nil
| y = if false:
|   1
| else if true:
|   2
| else:
|   3
| ;;
| print(y)
| # expect: 2
| z = match 1:
|   1 => if false: 0 else: 1 ;;
|   _ => 2
| ;;
| print(z)
| # expect: 1
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
