==== tests/lang/time_rules.fung
-- stdout
| # time.parse without a format reads RFC 3339 strictly; a numeric offset stays as written.
| import time
| print(time.parse("2026-10-17T10:02:03Z"), time.parse("2026-10-17t10:02:03.5z"), time.parse("2026-10-17T10:02:03+08:27:52"))
| # expect: Ok(2026-10-17T10:02:03Z) Ok(2026-10-17T10:02:03.5Z) Ok(2026-10-17T10:02:03+08:27:52)
| lax = ["2026-10-17T1:02:03Z", "2026-10-17T10:02:03,5Z", "2026-10-17T10:02:03+24:00", "2026-10-17T10:02:03.1234567891Z", "2026-10-17 10:02:03Z"]
| print(lax |> map(s => is_err(time.parse(s))))
| # expect: [true, true, true, true, true]
| print(time.parse("2026-13-01T00:00:00Z"))
| # expect: Err({kind: 'parse, message: "cannot parse `2026-13-01T00:00:00Z` as a time: month out of range"})
| print(time.parse("2026-02-30T00:00:00Z"))
| # expect: Err({kind: 'parse, message: "cannot parse `2026-02-30T00:00:00Z` as a time: day out of range"})
| print(time.format(time.parse("2026-10-17T10:02:03+09:00")!, "Z"))
| # expect: +09:00
| print(time.parse("Foo 1", "MMM D"))
| # expect: Err({kind: 'parse, message: "cannot parse `Foo 1` as a time: expected a month name like Oct"})
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== tests/lang/top_constants.fung
-- stdout
| # A top-level name assigned once is a constant: top-level fns see it
| # when they are called (03).
| limit = 3
| names = ["a", "b"]
| counter = atom(0)
| fn ok(n) = n <= limit
| fn bump() = swap(counter, x => x + 1)
| fn first() = names[0]
| fn deep() = map([1, 5], x => ok(x))
| print(ok(2), ok(4), first(), deep())
| # expect: true false a [true, false]
| bump()
| bump()
| print(deref(counter))
| # expect: 2
| (lo, hi) = (1, 9)
| fn within(n) = lo <= n and n <= hi
| print(within(5), within(10))
| # expect: true false
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/try_map.fung
-- stdout
| # try_map stops at the first Err (03).
| print(["1", "2", "3"] |> try_map(s => parse_int(s)))
| # expect: Ok([1, 2, 3])
| calls = atom(0)
| parse = fn(s):
|   swap(calls, n => n + 1)
|   parse_int(s)
| ;;
| print(try_map(["1", "x", "3"], parse))
| # expect: Err({kind: 'parse, message: "not an integer: x"})
| print(deref(calls))
| # expect: 2
| print(try_map([], parse), try_map(1..=2, n => Ok(n * 10)))
| # expect: Ok([]) Ok([10, 20])
|
| # The usual shape: the first failure goes to the caller with ?.
| fn total(xs) = Ok(sum(try_map(xs, parse_int)?))
| print(total(["4", "5"]), total(["4", "y"]))
| # expect: Ok(9) Err({kind: 'parse, message: "not an integer: y"})
|
| # A list of results made before: r => r.
| print(try_map([Ok(1), Ok(2)], r => r))
| # expect: Ok([1, 2])
|
| # ? stays fine in a named fn handed to map, and in other lambdas.
| fn twice(s) = Ok(parse_int(s)? * 2)
| print(map(["1", "x"], twice))
| # expect: [Ok(2), Err({kind: 'parse, message: "not an integer: x"})]
| print(with(() => "7", s => Ok(parse_int(s)?), _ => nil))
| # expect: Ok(7)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0

==== tests/lang/types_enum.fung
-- stdout
| # A tag name as a type takes the tag without values too (07): an enum.
| type color = Red | Green | Blue
| fn hex(c: color) -> str = match c:
|   Red   => "#f00"
|   Green => "#0f0"
|   Blue  => "#00f"
| ;;
| print(hex(Green), hex('Blue))
| # expect: #0f0 #00f
| fn area(s: Circle | Square) -> num = match s:
|   Circle(r) => 3 * r * r
|   Square(a) => a * a
| ;;
| print(area(Circle(1)), area(Square(2)))
| # expect: 3 4
| print(type_of(Red))
| # expect: 'sym
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/types_more.fung
-- stdout
| # `file` is a type name (07), `...rest` may say `list`, and a function
| # with a return type calls itself in tail position without a stack (03).
| import fs
| fn kind(f: file) = type_of(f)
| print(fs.with_temp_dir(fn(d):
|   fs.with_open("${d}/x", f => kind(f), mode: 'write)
| ;;))
| # expect: 'file
| fn count(...xs: list) -> int = len(xs)
| print(count(1, 2, 3))
| # expect: 3
| fn deep(n, acc) -> int = n == 0 then acc else deep(n - 1, acc + 1)
| print(deep(300000, 0))
| # expect: 300000
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/underscore_assign.fung
-- stdout
| _ = print("side")
| print(1)
| # expect: side
| # expect: 1
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/unused_var_silent_in_run.fung
-- stdout
| fn f():
|   unused = 1
|   2
| ;;
| print(f())
| # expect: 2
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/with.fung
-- stdout
| # The release of a with runs whatever the use did.
| print(with(() => "r", r => r + "!", r => print("released", r)))
| print(with(() => 1, r => Err('x), r => print("released")))
| # expect: released r
| # expect: r!
| # expect: released
| # expect: Err('x)
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
==== tests/lang/with_exit.fung
-- stdout
| # Every release runs after exit(n), innermost first.
| with(() => "outer", fn(_):
|   with(() => "inner", fn(_):
|     print("body")
|     exit(3)
|     print("unreachable")
|   ;;, r => print("released", r))
| ;;, r => print("released", r))
| # expect: body
| # expect: released inner
| # expect: released outer
| # expect-exit: 3
-- stderr
-- exit: 0
